# DawnTrader Phase History

> **Purpose**: Chronological record of how DawnTrader evolved by phase, including batch mapping and the major architectural milestones each phase delivered.
> **Updated by**: Langston (compiled from current governance docs, frozen snapshot history, and the accessible canonical-history bundle)
> **Location**: `1-system-manual/PHASE_HISTORY.md`
>
> **Source note**: The historical bundle path provided in the assignment did not exist exactly as written. The accessible canonical context used for pre-governance reconstruction was: `DawnTrader_Canonical_Context_v2025-12-13/`.

## Phase 12 — Cleanup & Foundation

### Purpose
Phase 12 was the disciplined post-audit cleanup program that turned DawnTrader from a contaminated hybrid of old and new systems into a governable codebase. It had three major themes: urgent math/security corrections, dead-code/legacy removal, and pipeline-unification work.

### 12.1 — Critical Math & Security Fixes
Phase 12.1 corrected foundational defects before deeper architecture changes began.

- **Batch 1 / 1B** fixed BUG-004 by restoring real geometric directional integrity instead of synthetic confidence-derived DI and then documented that fix in governance.
- **Batch 2 / 2B** resolved the dual-friction split-brain problem (RISK-009) by replacing flat friction shortcuts with the canonical round-trip cost model and then recording that fix across the governance stack.
- **Batch 3 / 3B** combined security hardening, simulated-price cleanup, and RiskManager comment/stub cleanup into a single multi-directive closure wave.

### 12.2 — Dead Code Purge
Phase 12.2 was the large legacy-removal campaign.

- **Batch 4 / 4B** removed NLAI.
- **Batches 5 through 7B** dismantled the Walter/Bob/Cortex cluster in stages: safe deletions first, then importer/frontend cleanup, then high-complexity surgery and hotfixes.
- **Batches 8 and 9** removed LATTi residuals, the orphaned DHMA strategy module, dead frontend pages, and the obsolete MarketScanner class.
- **Batches 10 and 11** cleaned dead Walter-era learning services, removed the old goal-alignment gate system, and removed deprecated friction functions.

This phase materially reduced the blast radius of later architecture work and created a cleaner foundation for MCE and Phase 14.x.

### 12.3 — Pipeline Unification
Phase 12.3 unified core decision authority.

- **Batch 12 / 12B** staged and documented the 17-strategy expansion specification.
- **Batch 13 / 13B** implemented regime authority resolution, confidence authority cleanup, and strategy-routing expansion so the trading pipeline was operating off one coherent control path instead of multiple competing authorities.

### Why Phase 12 Mattered
Without Phase 12, later work like MCE installation and dual-path scanning would have been built on top of contradictory math, obsolete services, and contaminated routing logic. Phase 12 was the cleanup-and-unification bridge that made Phase 13 and 14 possible.

---

## Phase 13 — MCE Installation + L12/L20 Removal

### Purpose
Phase 13 installed the Market Context Engine (MCE) as the centralized regime and indicator authority, while removing a large legacy cluster that had become redundant or misleading.

### Primary Batch
- **Batch 14**

### What Landed
- MCE became the central authority for market context rather than one participant among many competing engines.
- Legacy L12/L20 cluster files were removed.
- Strategy enum/state handling was expanded and aligned with the newer strategy architecture.
- Major consumers such as orchestration and simulation paths were rewired around the new context authority.

### Why It Mattered
Phase 13 was the architecture pivot that made the later dual-path and diagnostics work tractable. Once MCE became the clear source of truth for market context, it became possible to build more advanced filter/routing systems without compounding regime-authority drift.

---

## Phase 14 — VTS Real Calculations / DBS / Regime Transition

### Purpose
Phase 14 advanced the simulation and execution realism path. It pushed the system from broad cleanup into practical trading-pipeline refinement: real VTS calculations, regime-name/regime-model cleanup, DBS handling, and then later branching into the 14.1–14.6 sub-phases.

### Primary Batches
- **Batch 15**
- **HF6**
- **HF7**

### What Landed
- VTS real-calculation work began in earnest.
- Regime rename/cleanup work advanced and then required completion and recalibration through hotfixes.
- DBS was incorporated strongly enough that later governance treats Phase 14.2 as effectively complete/already implemented.

### Why It Split
Phase 14 proved too broad to remain a single undifferentiated phase. The work naturally branched into sub-phases such as 14.1 (VTS throughput / IMF / regime closure), 14.3 (short trading), 14.5 (dual-path pattern scanning), and 14.6 (X Stocks).

---

## Phase 14.1 — VTS Throughput, Regime, IMF, Column Fixes

### Purpose
Phase 14.1 closed the immediate operational gaps left after the initial Phase 14 work. This phase was about making the VTS path more usable, cleaning up residual regime issues, and finishing IMF/column/governance-gate work that surfaced once the first real-calculation wave landed.

### Primary Delivery Path
- **HF8** — VTS throughput fixes
- **HF9** — column fix, governance gate, DSS deletion, VTS IMF

### Supporting Scoped Batches
- **Batch 16** and **Batch 17** captured planned work that was later absorbed into HF8/HF9 rather than persisting as the final canonical delivery artifacts.

### Outcome
Phase 14.1 is treated as complete. Phase 14.1B was later eliminated as its own lane, with the relevant work absorbed into the hotfix path rather than preserved as a separate enduring phase.

---

## Phase 14.5 — Dual-Path Pattern Scanning + Merit-Based Ranking + Filter Diagnostics

### Overall Purpose
Transform DawnTrader from a single-path quant-first system into a dual-path architecture where both quantitative strategies and candlestick-pattern strategies can operate across the scanned universe. Replace arbitrary pair limiting with merit-based ranking, migrate filters toward DB-driven governance, and expose the filter/evaluation pipeline through diagnostics.

**Duration**: 2026-03-18 through 2026-03-22 (Batch 19 through Batch 19K GOV)

### 14.5.1 — Core Dual-Path Architecture (Batch 19)
- Dual-path pattern scanning introduced alongside the quant path.
- Merit-based rankingScore ordering replaced crude queue behavior.
- MCE global regime overlay and source-pool identity were added so downstream systems could distinguish path origin and context.

### 14.5.2 — Deferred/VTS Pattern-Pool Work (Batch 19C, Batch 19E)
- Deferred items from Batch 19 were completed.
- VTS pattern-pool persistence and frontend visibility were added, including sourcePool-aware state handling.
- Pattern-path visibility improved both in storage and in the UI.

### 14.5.3 — DB-Driven Filter Architecture Completion (Batch 19F + hotfixes)
- Filter architecture moved further into database-driven configuration.
- Hybrid confluence and ABCD-pattern work landed.
- Follow-up hotfixes repaired IMF/threshold/regime edge cases exposed by the architecture shift.

### 14.5.4 — 4-Path Filter Architecture (Batch 19G + hotfixes)
- FX5 began reading filter thresholds from the database instead of stale hardcoded constants.
- VTS deduplication and pattern-scanning visibility improved.
- The system moved toward a transparent 4-path filter model rather than mixed hidden logic.

### 14.5.5 — VN / DI Quantitative Cleanup (19G VN family + 19G DI)
- VN was revised to a log-returns MAD/median formulation.
- Thresholds were recalibrated from observed behavior.
- DI moved to a rolling 48-candle window to remain analytically useful.
- Deprecated VN/DI constants and hidden split-brain behavior were removed or reduced.

### 14.5.6 — Filter Diagnostics (19H, 19I, 19J)
- Batch 19H introduced the Filter Pipeline Diagnostics tab.
- Batch 19I added number formatting, faster refresh, and richer VTS evaluation metrics.
- Batch 19J changed VTS breakdown reporting from last-cycle-only to 24-hour rolling aggregation.

### 14.5.7 — Governance Overhaul (19H GOV, 19K GOV)
- Governance and workflow ownership were updated around the 14.5 delivery chain.
- CCPI was rewritten with a strong essentials section.
- `BATCH_CATALOG.md` and `PHASE_HISTORY.md` became new canonical governance docs.
- The old directives workflow was archived into `directives-archive/`.

### Outcome
Phase 14.5 is treated as fully complete in the current governance state. It is one of the most consequential architecture/program phases in DawnTrader because it fundamentally changed how pairs are filtered, ranked, evaluated, and explained.

---

## Phase 14.6 — Strategy-Family Filter Profiles + Diagnostics Data Truth (Batches 20-39)

### Purpose
Implement strategy-family-aware filtering, establish data truth in filter diagnostics, and deploy the family-qualified candidate identity model. Each strategy family gets IMF thresholds tuned to its market environment. Filter pipeline metrics become fully reconcilable.

### Included Batches
- **Batch 20** (2026-03-23) — Pre-implementation audit. Architecture B selected. DI threshold recalibration identified.
- **Batch 21** (2026-03-23) — Telemetry & Calibration Scaffolding.
- **Batch 22** (2026-03-23) — Architecture B Implementation. 4 family IMF filter paths.
- **Batch 22 HF-HF7** (2026-03-23) — Post-deployment hotfixes (7 total).
- **Batch 23** (2026-03-24) — DI Threshold Calibration + Null Reason Expansion.
- **Batch 23 HF** (2026-03-24) — Empirical DI recalibration (crypto DI distribution 3-20).
- **Batch 24** (2026-03-24) — Filter Diagnostics data truth + per-pool counter split.
- **Batch 25** (2026-03-25) — Counter reset + pattern null split + all rejection categories.
- **Batch 26** (2026-03-25) — Counter truth fixes (ADX guard, family filter, Net EV reorder).
- **Batch 27** (2026-03-26) — Counter fixes + investigation resolutions.
- **Batch 28** (2026-03-26) — Pattern-path DI threshold adjustment.
- **Batch 29** (2026-03-26) — UI layout + labeling fixes.
- **Batch 36** (2026-03-27) — Diagnostics correctness fixes (sourcePool on closedTradeRecord, DI aggregation).
- **Batch 37** (2026-03-27) — Source pool family-qualified identity model. Replaced generic `quant` with `quant-trend/reversal/breakout/oscillation`. 10 changes across 13 files. Reconciliation proof verified.
- **Batch 38** (2026-03-27) — 3-layer null taxonomy (Setup Nulls A-F, Routing/Path Failures, Post-Signal Rejections). Signals rejected counter fix.
- **Batch 39** (2026-03-27) — Pipeline Summary Table with counting basis labels. Family label polish.

### Key Architecture Decisions
- **Architecture B (brute-force fan-out)** over Architecture A (early MCE)
- **Family-qualified candidate identity** — `quant-trend`, `quant-reversal`, `quant-breakout`, `quant-oscillation` replace generic `quant`
- **Total quant survivors = sum of family survivors** (not deduplicated by symbol)
- **No hidden fallbacks** — explicit family filtering, CONFIG_MISSING error if family path absent
- **3-layer null taxonomy** — Setup Nulls (data/context), Routing/Path Failures, Post-Signal Rejections
- **Pipeline Summary Table** — full pipeline flow with counting basis labels at top of Filter Diagnostics

### Outcome
Phase 14.6 is COMPLETE. The system now has family-qualified candidate identity, reconcilable pipeline metrics, and transparent filter diagnostics. Next: Phase 15 (X Stocks + Perpetual Futures).

### Outcome
This inter-phase lane created the stable baseline that Phase 14.5 was built on.

## Pre-Governance History (Phases 1-11)

> **Reconstruction basis**: `bridge/canonical/` materials in the repo, especially `DawnTrader_Complete_Project_History.md`, `Phase_8_Implementation_History.md`, `Phase_9_Implementation_History.md`, `Phase_10_Implementation_History.md`, and `Phase_11_Implementation_History.md`.
>
> **Important**: These phases predate the current governance/batch-documentation system, so the summaries below are historical reconstructions rather than batch-level records.

### Phases 1-3 — Initial Platform Build + Walter Era
The earliest DawnTrader build established the core V1 trading platform: FX5 scanning, a 9-strategy engine, RTB queuing, paper-trading lifecycle, and a Stage-3 event-store style truth engine. During this same era, Walter was integrated as an OpenAI-backed AI sysadmin/advisory copilot with memory, intent, reasoning, and expert-corpus components.

### Phases 3-4 — Walter Sidelined, LATTi Created
Walter was removed from the real-time path because API latency, throttling, and cost made him unsuitable for live-loop decision support. LATTi/Lottie was then created as a local, embedded, passive-only telemetry/tuning layer, establishing the long-running split between offline strategic AI guidance and local runtime behavior.

### Phases 4-5 — Failed V2 Attempt
A full V2 rewrite was attempted and failed catastrophically. The historical record makes clear that this period damaged continuity rather than improving it, and it directly motivated the later restoration and rebuild discipline.

### Phases 5-7 — Restoration and Early Refactoring
After the failed V2 path, DawnTrader went through restoration and structured cleanup to recover the working V1 system. This was the bridge into the more explicitly documented Phase 8.x era.

### Phase 8 — Foundation Repairs, REB Restoration, End-to-End Pipeline Recovery
Phase 8’s stated mission was to fix and complete the paper-mode trading engine without Lottie active in the loop. The canonical Phase 8 history breaks the work into:

- **8.1** — accounting-model / FX5 output repairs
- **8.2** — passive-learning isolation
- **8.3** — scan-cadence repair
- **8.4** — breakdown-accuracy fixes
- **8.5** — batch-selection repair
- **8.6** — top-end rotation + UI integration
- **8.7** — reactivation of unused filters

A GitHub incident on **2025-11-20** erased roughly 10-14 days of work and triggered the **REB** (rebuild) program. The historical sequence then moved through:
- **REB 1.0-2.12F** — emergency restoration of lost functionality
- **8.8.1** — scanner-output audit
- **8.8.2** — signal-engine audit
- **8.8.3** — end-to-end trading-pipeline restoration

By the end of Phase 8.8.3, DawnTrader again had a functioning end-to-end scanner → signal → RTB → paper-trade pipeline.

### Phase 9 — Math Core Finalization
Phase 9’s mission was to finalize DawnTrader’s mathematical core and establish single sources of truth for quantitative calculations.

Key documented outcomes from the canonical Phase 9 history:
- **9.0-FP** — deprecated-file quarantine and foundation prep
- **9.1-9.5** — `SYSTEM_GUARDS` established as the central store for math constants and thresholds
- **9.6** — sim-to-live parity test suite created
- **9.7** — Guardrails v2 migration from dollar-based to percentage-based risk logic
- **9.8** — validation and legacy purge
- **9.9** — CWQI net expectancy and friction standardization

In practical terms, Phase 9 centralized scoring/risk constants, hardened parity between simulation and live math, and moved the system toward a more disciplined quantitative foundation.

### Phase 10 — Hybrid Alpha Pattern Engine & Trade Lifecycle Modernization
Phase 10’s mission was to build the **Hybrid Alpha Pattern Engine** and modernize the trade lifecycle for production readiness.

The canonical Phase 10 history records:
- **10.0-10.3** — hybrid integration of quant + pattern + ML-style inputs
- **10.4-10.6** — multi-timeframe expansion (1H / 15m / 5m cascade logic)
- **10.7** — adaptive scanning intelligence
- **10.8** — math-core harmonization
- **10.9** — trade-lifecycle modernization

This phase introduced ensemble/hybrid concepts, expanded multi-timeframe logic, and modernized execution-lifecycle handling — much of the conceptual groundwork that later evolved into the more heavily governed Phase 11-14 architecture.

### Phase 11 — Mathematical & Operational Hardening
Phase 11 is the richest pre-governance phase in the canonical bridge material. Its implementation history describes the phase as the hardening layer spanning metric consolidation through authority unification and legacy decommission.

Major directive families documented there:
- **11.0** — metric-engine consolidation and FinalScore as the canonical quality metric
- **11.1** — canonical regime-strategy mapping
- **11.2** — VTS modernization and regime-driven simulation
- **11.3** — adaptive scanning intelligence
- **11.4** — indicators and analytics hardening
- **11.5** — math / macro / regime synchronization
- **11.6** — data purge and ML reset
- **11.7** — regime archive and telemetry infrastructure
- **11.8 / 11.8C** — authority unification and legacy decommission

The Executive Summary for Phase 11 explicitly highlights:
- unified FinalScore replacing legacy CWQI/NGC/ProfitRate paths,
- canonical regime-strategy relationships,
- VTS modernization,
- adaptive scanning,
- regime archive infrastructure,
- authority unification,
- and decommissioning of legacy systems like LATTi / Goals ML / ARA / Strategy Presets.

This is the clearest precursor to the modern audited/governed DawnTrader architecture. The later 2026 roadmap’s “Phase 11 Finalization” language indicates that although major Phase 11 foundations were built, some parts of the finalization/authority framework remained unfinished when the Phase 12+ governance era began.

### Historical Significance of Phases 1-11
The pre-governance era established nearly all of DawnTrader’s core ambitions: high-volume scanning, strategy orchestration, paper-trading lifecycle, AI-adjacent advisory/tuning layers, hybrid-pattern experimentation, and authority/math unification. It also created much of the technical debt, legacy overlap, and split-brain behavior that Phases 12 through 14.5 later had to unwind, standardize, or formally retire.

**Phase 15c follow-up sub-batch 2026-04-30 afternoon (B73.2 + Factor Calibration UI panel SHIPPED — variant-collapse fix + actual predictive analysis surface):**

- **Triggered** by Kyle observation 2026-04-30 afternoon that B73.1 morning fix shipped clean but ALL variants STILL collapsed to identical rows on every closed trade. Direct Kraken OHLC pull on AIXBT/USD diagnosis trade: max bar high was +0.5% above entry, BE trigger threshold (entry + 1×ATR_proxy) was at +5.9% — no bar's high ever crossed the trigger, so no variant fired any level. Yet live trade DID latch BE — meaning live TEC saw sub-minute price movement that 1-min OHLC bars don't expose.
- **Diagnosis** (Langston cc-inbox #866): TWO separate problems requiring TWO separate fixes. Q1 (headline "does live BE-stop convert TPs to BEs?") needs extended OHLC window past actualExit so Variants F/K can see post-exit reality. Q2 (variant parameter comparison) needs trigger thresholds consistent with bar-resolution data — bar-derived ATR.
- **Implementation** (`a98ce7ff`, PM2 #119): (a) Bar-derived ATR — recompute from 14 1-min bars BEFORE entry as 14-period TR average. Variant trigger thresholds use this in place of `atrAtOpen` proxy. Trade-off acknowledged: replay ATR ≠ live ATR; framework answers "what would variant X have done with bar-resolution thresholds" not "in live world." (b) Extended OHLC window from `exitTime + 1h` to `entryTime + maxHoldMs` (7d) regardless of actual exit. Pagination enabled (10080 candle cap, 14 batches × 720 candles, 500ms delay). Async fire-and-forget so the 7s pagination doesn't block trade-close persistence. (c) Both `atr_live` and `atr_bar_derived` logged in metadata of every B73 variant row for diagnostic validation. Wiped 180 useless inherited-only B73 rows from B73.1 window.
- **Plus Factor Calibration UI panel** — Kyle observation that pre-B67.5 the Factor Ablation Comparison panel was decoratively dead (all 4 factors with identical zero-counts because no consumer gates on confidence pre-B67.5). The underlying confidence-shift data IS captured per signal in `regime_factor_alternates.alternateDecision.confidence` — was just never surfaced. New `computeFactorCalibration()` aggregator + `GET /api/analytics/factor-calibration` endpoint + `FactorCalibrationSection` rendered above existing substrate panel. Two sub-views per factor: (1) Confidence-shift distribution (avg / max abs |REAL−ALT|, % at zero) shows whether each lever moves the confidence number; (2) Tertile WR + predictive lift = REAL spread − ALT spread shows whether high-confidence trades win more, AND whether each factor adds predictive value vs. the without-factor scenario. **Decision-grade gate n ≥ 150 per bucket per factor** per Langston cc-inbox #856 calibration check threshold. Existing Factor Ablation Comparison panel marked SUBSTRATE with explanatory pre-B67.5 note pointing readers to the new panel; stays in UI per Kyle directive 2026-04-30 afternoon (will be useful post-B67.5 + provides "data is flowing" sanity check).
- **Decision logic for the 14d window** now answerable mid-window: lift > +7pp with n ≥ 50/bucket and monotonic WR ordering = ship factor early; lift solidly negative with n ≥ 50/bucket = drop factor early. Panel updates every minute.

**Original B73 data-layer entry:**
- **B73 SHIPPED 2026-04-29** (commit `a747b646`, PM2 restart #115). Observation-only ablation framework that records what 12 BE-stop / trailing-stop variants WOULD have done on every closed VTS trade. Triggered by Kyle's 7d closed-trades review showing 509 BE_STOP (44%) vs 22 TP (2%); long winning streaks gone. Counterfactual analysis on n=87 trades with `originalStopPrice` populated showed 18.4% would have hit TP first (avg +9%) without BE-stop, but n too small to act on alone. B73 builds the multi-week observation framework. **What ships in this commit (data layer):** new `exit_strategy_alternates` table (parallel to B67.0's `regime_factor_alternates`), `exit-strategy-replay.ts` (12 variant evaluators with simplified trailing state machine), `exit-strategy-replay-service.ts` (orchestrator: 1-min OHLC fetch + bulk-insert; async fire-and-forget), `vts-service.persistRealPriceTrade` hook. 13 module_constants in new `exit_strategy_replay` module. Variant A baseline anchors on `b73_baseline_*` snapshot constants (NOT live `trailing_exit`) so paired-diff Sharpe stays valid across TEC tuning. Sharpe-like selection metric pre-registered. n=200 total + n=50 per-regime minimum for declaring a winner. Zero contamination with B67 calibration window (exits unchanged). Paper-execution-engine hook intentionally NOT wired (active trading OFF; B67 symmetry). **Tomorrow follow-up:** API endpoint + UI panel + unit tests. v2: real ATR plumbing, b73_variant_l_target_lock_r module_constant, gap_bar metadata flag. Langston-approved Steps 1/2/4 cc-inbox #861/#862/#863. Variants: A baseline / B ATR-padded BE+ / C higher BE trigger / D trailing instead of BE / E vol-conditional skip / F NO BE / G baseline trailing / H tighter trail / I looser trail / J no trailing / K NO BE + NO trail / L BE+pad and looser trail.
- **Calibration window unaffected.** B73 runs in parallel with B67 calibration; observation surface only.

**Phase 24 INFRASTRUCTURE HARDENING — post-incident retrofit triggered by B79.TEC failure mode (2026-05-15 → 2026-05-17):**

This block documents why B-NEW-40 was necessary and what production failure made it necessary. Preserved for future debugging context: if a similar `TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED` pattern (or a new variant) ever surfaces again, this is the prior-art trail.

**Trigger:** two `TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED` incidents on staging within 18 hours (2026-05-15 17:13 UTC and 2026-05-16 11:14 UTC) blocked the VTS exit loop, halted ablation emissions, and required PM2 restart (#288, #289) each time. Same root cause both times — recurring, not transient.

**Diagnosis (B-NEW-40 pre-audit, Langston rev1 + rev2 reviews):** two stacked contributors.

1. **Network-layer cause (pre-existing, B79.TEC didn't introduce it).** Silent TCP path death between Hetzner Falkenstein and Supabase Frankfurt. Pre-May 8 logs show heartbeat-cycle slowdowns of 14.9s and 96.9s outliers throughout April; the network condition existed before B79.TEC. The default `pg-pool` config has `keepAlive: false`, so the OS never probes idle sockets; dead-but-ESTABLISHED connections stay in the pool and get reused for queries that then hang indefinitely (no socket error, no statement timeout — the write lands in the local kernel buffer, the response never returns).

2. **Code-architecture amplifier (B79.TEC, 2026-05-08, commit `01fa39912`).** The per-asset-class fire-and-forget refresh pattern with `tecConfigRefreshInFlight` Map coalescer + 5-min staleness ceiling converts a single hung promise into a permanent fail-closed for the rest of process lifetime. Smoking gun: 0 `[TEC_REFRESH_FAIL]` events across 4832 `[TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED]` events (the `.catch` handler never executes because the promise neither resolves nor rejects). Architectural fingerprint of "Map entry stuck, every read re-evaluates the same staleness check against the same fixed timestamp": exact-duplicate-duration clustering (4 events at precisely 96,983ms, 4 events at 8,943ms) in post-May 8 logs.

**Pre-B79.TEC vs post-B79.TEC blast radius shift:**

| Aspect | Old (pre-2026-05-08) | New (post-B79.TEC, pre-B-NEW-40) |
|---|---|---|
| Pattern | `await getModuleConstants(...)` inline in try/catch | Fire-and-forget background refresh via `tecConfigRefreshInFlight` Map |
| If query rejects cleanly | `.catch` returns cached defaults | `.catch` increments fail counter; cache continues to be returned |
| If query HANGS | Caller hangs too; orchestrator cycle stalls one tick; next cycle starts fresh and recovers | Map entry traps the hung promise forever; no future refresh scheduled; at 5min ceiling, `resolveTECConfig` throws `TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED` permanently until PM2 restart |

The network condition was the same in both eras. B79.TEC changed how the system reacted to it from "one-tick stall, auto-recover" to "permanent fail-closed, requires manual restart."

**Why B-NEW-40 was necessary, not optional:** two PM2 restarts in 18 hours is operationally unacceptable. The pattern was recurring on a 12–18h cadence; left in place, the system would have continued to require manual operator intervention at unpredictable times. NO PATCHES doctrine (CLAUDE.md §5 #15) required closing both contributors structurally, not papering over with a cron-based auto-restart.

| Batch | Date | Commit | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| **B-NEW-40** | 2026-05-17 | ✅ CLOSED (Kyle ack 2026-05-17). Commits `6a70b45c4` + `62890eaf0` + `5f156c297`. Deploy 12:46:47Z, PM2 #290. 14-day soak armed (alert `b83b1e4b`, fires 2026-05-31T12:46:47Z). | **pg pool keepalive + TEC refresh timeout — silent-TCP-death root-cause fix.** Five layers in one batch: (1) `server/db.ts` pool config (`keepAlive: true` + `keepAliveInitialDelayMillis: 10_000`, `query_timeout: 30_000`, `idleTimeoutMillis: 30_000`, explicit `max: 10`, `application_name: 'dawntrader_main'`); (2) `Promise.race(refresh, timeoutAfter45s)` fence around `refreshTECConfigForClass` so the in-flight Map always releases (45s budget = pool `query_timeout` 30s + 15s slack); (3) new `/api/diagnostics/tec-config` endpoint (read-only, auth-gated) surfacing per-class refresh state + Central Clock health; (4) hostile-scenario test asserting 5 conditions (Map releases ≤45s+ε, fail counter increments, `TEC_REFRESH_TIMEOUT` logs exactly once, cached config returned until 5min, throw past 5min); (5) `tec-pg-capture` systemd unit on staging adds `ss -tnpi` snapshot per tick after stale-fail trigger (direct TCP socket evidence at incident time). Central Clock audit (Kyle directive 2026-05-17): zero new recurring schedules, zero competing timers — additions are kernel-layer (TCP keepalive), pg-pool-internal (query_timeout, idle reaping), per-call one-shot (Promise.race), or event-driven (ss capture). SIM governance gap closed in same batch — new section in SYSTEM_IMPACT_MAP.md covers the B79.TEC config-cache subsystem (cache maps, `primeTECConfig` bootstrap, `hasExplicitAssetClassRow` invariant, 45s timeout fence, canonical log signatures, bidirectional link with `server/db.ts` pool entry). Langston Step 1 + Step 2 sign-off APPROVED 2026-05-17 with 5 corrections applied. Verification: 14-day staging soak; zero `TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED` events = cause closed. Crypto + xstock regression NONE by-construction (additive resilience, per-call bounds, read-only diagnostic). Cross-references: `INFRA-2026-05-17-A` in CHANGES_AND_FIXES.md, "Recent Additions (B-NEW-40)" section in SYSTEM_IMPACT_MAP.md, `B_NEW_40_SCOPE.md` + `B_NEW_40_PRE_AUDIT.md`. |

**Lessons captured for future debugging context:**

1. **Fire-and-forget patterns must have a timeout escape hatch.** Any `await someAsyncOp()` chained with `.catch().finally()` that relies on the awaited promise eventually settling will silently lock its surrounding coalescer/in-flight tracker if the underlying promise hangs. The fix is `Promise.race([op, timeout])`, never just `op`. This is a general pattern, not specific to TEC.

2. **pg-pool's default `keepAlive: false` is dangerous on long-lived Node processes with cross-region database connections.** SO_KEEPALIVE must be explicitly enabled, and `keepAliveInitialDelayMillis` set short enough (10s is the empirical sweet spot) to actually detect dead sockets within a useful window — Linux default 2-hour `tcp_keepalive_time` is unusable.

3. **"Zero log events for the catch handler" is a smoking gun for a hung promise.** If a `.catch` clause never executes despite thousands of downstream symptoms, the underlying promise is neither resolving nor rejecting. That's not a "rare race"; that's a hung await on a dead I/O channel.

4. **Exact-duplicate-duration log clustering is the fingerprint of "trapped Map entry, every reader re-evaluates the same fixed check."** Distinct from a recurring network blip, which produces a distribution of durations.

5. **Architecture refactors that decouple a sync hang from its caller can convert benign one-tick stalls into permanent fail-closed states.** B79.TEC's refactor was correct in its design intent (boot-prime, per-class isolation, fail-closed safety), but the in-flight Map's lack of an external timeout was the regression. Architecture reviews going forward should ask: "if the awaited operation hangs forever, does any state in this code path remain trapped?"

6. **Re-deploy staging as the LAST step of any session that ships a new UI surface — not just at the initial deploy point.** B-NEW-40 deployed at 2026-05-17T12:46:47Z (PM2 #290) included the `authFetch`→`apiFetch` hotfix (commits `6a70b45c4` + `62890eaf0` both pre-deploy). Step 7 first-pass UI verification via Claude-in-Chrome confirmed the System Alerts tab rendered correctly at the time. However, Kyle's later browser session at end-of-day reported the tab not visible. The fresh re-deploy at 2026-05-17T16:11Z (PM2 #291) resolved it cleanly; Claude-in-Chrome re-verified the tab now renders with the soak alert correctly populated. Root cause not isolated with certainty — likely Kyle's browser cached older HTML predating the deploy, but other transient asset-mismatch causes are possible. **Process correction:** when ANY hotfix follows the initial deploy in the same session, OR when the session continues with subsequent work (other batches, governance changes) that doesn't touch staging-side code, **re-run `git pull && npm run build && pm2 restart dawntrader` as the final action before declaring UI surfaces "live to Kyle."** A separate Claude-in-Chrome verification at end-of-session — using the same URL Kyle would type into his browser — also helps catch what Kyle would actually see. The Step 7 verification I did intra-session was valid for the verification timestamp but not predictive of what Kyle would see hours later.

**Lessons captured from B-NEW-41:**

1. **Smoke-test format-handling claims with real production audio, not just bundled samples.** Pre-audit §3.2 assumed whisper-cli handled Ogg natively; bundled `jfk.wav` smoke test was WAV which masked the limitation. First real voice note from Kyle exposed the gap.

2. **Implicit scope contraction at implementation time creates governance debt.** B67.5/B68.5 sustainability gate originally framed broader than confidence-modification; shipped scope contracted without explicit deferral tag. The 2026-05-17 surfacing required a multi-session research effort that could have been avoided if the contraction had been tagged as Phase-19-deferred at ship time. Captured in `DESIGN-2026-05-17-A` in CHANGES_AND_FIXES.

3. **Single-claude-at-a-time invariant requires unified task queue across all inbound types.** Voice + text routing through separate paths in langston-bridge would have produced two concurrent `claude --session-id <same UUID>` subprocesses → session state corruption. Step 2 Rev 1 from Langston caught this before implementation.

4. **Defense-in-depth on SSH grants — choose `deploy` over `root` when grantee's needs are read-only.** Original B-NEW-41 Q5 was `root` for Langston; Langston pushed back during Step 1 review; Kyle reconsidered to `deploy` with `from="<IP>"` IP restriction. Compromise of Helsinki box now gives deploy-level read access on staging, NOT root.

5. **Step 7 (Kyle-in-the-loop verification) reveals real-world failure modes the pre-audit cannot catch.** Three hotfixes required during Step 7 because actual voice notes from real Telegram exercises code paths that synthetic tests don't. Voice transcription is now operational, but the hotfix-during-Step-7 pattern is worth flagging — future similar batches should plan for it.

Awaiting Kyle authorization to proceed to B-NEW-42b Step 3 implementation. |

**Lessons captured from B-NEW-42:**

1. **Predicted-outcome analysis in pre-audit is high-value when it sets expectations correctly but high-risk when the host-environment gates short-circuit tests.** Pre-audit §3.1 predicted "forward-split test LIKELY FAILS" — confirmed. But initial test run with `assetClass='xstock_spot'` and host clock on Sunday produced false-pass: every test short-circuited via `isXstockMarketOpenUTC` weekend gate before the bug surface was reached. Required `vi.mock('../../asset_classes/xstock_spot/market-hours.js')` to isolate the structural TEC gap from the orthogonal weekend safety. Both tests now carry a multi-line comment block at the top explaining the mock + the silent-rot failure mode if it's removed without time-injection replacement.

2. **Statement-timeout (B-NEW-40-enforced 30s pool-level) constrains archive-scan SQL more than expected.** Pass B (OHLC consecutive-bar step-change) timed out on the 14.2M-row OHLC table even with `SET statement_timeout=0`. The pool's `query_timeout: 30_000` from B-NEW-40 fires before the SET applies in some pgbouncer-style routing scenarios. Worked around by partitioned-table direct query + date filtering. Future audit-scan scripts should default to partition-direct + date-filtered patterns instead of parent-table scans.

3. **Empirical archive evidence reframes structural fixes from "theoretical" to "operationally urgent."** Audit halt-scan surfaced 462 candidate halt-with-resume-gap events in 7 days (66/day average, max 4.6%). Without that empirical grounding, the halt-detector might have been deferred or under-prioritized. Future fixes designed against archive-observed-behavior carry stronger ship-priority signal than fixes designed against pure structural arguments.

4. **Symbol-format divergence between display form and storage form bit the audit.** Pre-audit assumed dividend candidate symbols would be stored as `KOx/USD` (matching `XSTOCK_SPOT_DISPLAY` regex). Actual archive stores them as `KO/USD` (no `x` suffix). First dividend scan returned 0 universe hits; corrected on second pass after listing actual stored symbols. Lesson: when querying archive against a candidate-symbol list, verify the actual storage form via a `SELECT DISTINCT symbol` quick-check before running the main scan.

5. **Fork-not-expand discipline (Langston rev2 §Q3 + #15 NO PATCHES) held under temptation.** When the audit confirmed DIRTY, the natural temptation was to fold the fix into B-NEW-42 (one less batch round-trip, faster ship). Discipline held: B-NEW-42 closes as audit-only, B-NEW-42b spawned with its own scope + design + Langston code review + verification. Treating the discovered-gap entry as B-NEW-42 governance and the fix entry as B-NEW-42b governance keeps the catalog readable and the workflow clean.

**Lessons captured from B-PHASE-A2:**

1. **Constructor-option discriminator beats partition-key for two-singleton class patterns.** Initial design rev1 framed the two-store pattern as "instances sharing class shape." Langston Step 1 R4 review surfaced that `publishSnapshot()` behavior diverges between crypto and xstock modes (sector partition + dual floors only for xstock), requiring an explicit `mode` discriminator in the constructor. Final shape: `new DirectionalBiasStore({mode, assetClassForKnobs})`. Future-proofing for asset class 3: registry-of-stores keyed by asset class is a 15-min refactor when it lands; don't bake it now.

2. **Pre-audit code-level rigor catches design assumptions before implementation lands.** Kyle directive 2026-05-17 expanded the pre-audit from "doc-level summary" to "code-level review with embedded snippets per §6.5.0.a + full SIM cascade analysis." Result: caught the `getSectorCoverageFloor` silent-fallback pattern AND the volume column omission AND the slope semantics question — all surfaced at pre-audit code-trace stage, NOT at Step 4 review. Sub-task A would have shipped the silent fallback otherwise. Lesson: pre-audit is the cheapest place to find these issues; spend the time there.

3. **No silent fallbacks for DB-governed settings (CLAUDE.md §8 #10) — applied during Step 4 BLOCKER.** Sub-task A's first draft used try/catch with hardcoded 7 fallback for `sector_coverage_floor`. Langston Step 4 flagged as BLOCKER: "an operator deleting the seeded row should bring the system to a halt, not silently let it run with a hardcoded default." Strict `getCachedNumberRequired` matches `getGlobalDbsMinSampleCount` shape; missing row = loud crash, not silent degradation. Same pattern, same fail-mode, both knobs.

4. **Companion-doc spot-check pause is correct for high-judgment data deliverables.** Step 3 sub-task B's 265-entry sector mapping has many GICS judgment calls (GOOGL→XLC post-2018 reclass, AMZN→XLY despite AWS, MSTR→XLK + cryptoAdjacent flag, COIN→XLF + cryptoAdjacent). TypeScript hard-fail catches MISSING entries but not WRONG entries. Adding `xstock_sector_mappings_reference.md` as a separate companion doc with full per-entry rationale + GICS reclassification gotchas + 30-60min Langston spot-check ACK before the TypeScript-mapping commit hit `migration/aws-supabase` proved sound: zero mappings revised at spot-check; pattern locked for future high-judgment data PRs.

5. **The "synthesized neutral" graceful-degrade preserves backward compatibility cleanly.** xStock pairs with insufficient OHLC archive (<48 bars), ATR=0, or sector missing fall through to `propagatedDbs=undefined`; MCE non-crypto branch builds `{score:0, category:'NEUTRAL', sentinelZero:true}` exactly as before A.2. Lesson: when adding a new signal to a hot path, preserve the pre-existing fallback rather than removing it; new signal degrades cleanly to old behavior when its inputs are absent.

**Lessons captured from B-NEW-42b:**

1. **GDrive FUSE mount is a Langston-side single-point-of-hang.** Two Step 4 round-2 dispatches hung 30+ minutes each because Langston's claude-cli auto-exploration ran `cd /mnt/gdrive/... && git status` on the 10GB+ repo. The third dispatch (v3) embedded ALL the code-diff snippets inline + EXPLICIT "do NOT cd to gdrive" instruction. ACK'd in <1 minute. Lesson: when dispatching code reviews to Langston, embed essential diff content + explicitly disallow gdrive access. The repo IS accessible via SSH on staging (`ssh staging 'cd /home/deploy/dawntrader && git ...'`) which is fast — gdrive should NEVER be the access path during a review.

2. **Detector-owned cache cleaner than caller-side prev-tick propagation (Langston pre-audit rev1 #1 design refinement accepted).** Initial scope rev2 §2.2.3 specified caller-propagation of `prevPrice`/`prevTs` through `evaluateTECExit` / `shouldClosePosition` / `updatePosition`. Pre-audit §3 reconsidered: detector maintains its own `Map<symbol, lastTick>` cache; callers pass only `currentPrice` + `currentTs` (which they already have). Outcome structurally equivalent; caller-side surface unchanged; silent-disable failure mode (caller forgets to plumb prevPrice) structurally eliminated.

3. **Double-consultation per logical tick (Langston Step 4 BLOCKER 2) is a non-obvious state-machine bug.** Pre-Step-4-review code consulted the detector independently in `updatePosition` (target-lock decision) and `shouldClosePosition` (stop-check decision). Both within microseconds. The first consult advanced IDLE → DISCONTINUITY_ACTIVE; the second consult (same logical tick, identical currentPrice → |Δ%|=0 from resume) advanced DISCONTINUITY_ACTIVE → CLEARING. Intended 2-tick deferral collapsed to 1-tick. Fix: hoist consultation to ONE call per logical tick in `tec-evaluator.ts`; thread result down to both gate sites via parameter. Lesson worth capturing for any future TEC-integrated stateful sentinel — single-consult-per-tick is the architecturally clean pattern.

4. **Cold-start fail-safe-skip is a structural correctness property, not a "small" addition.** Initial scope rev2 had detector return `{active: false}` on cold-start (no prior tick = no signal); Langston rev1 #1 non-negotiably required `{active: true, kind: 'cold_start'}`. Reasoning: in absence of certainty about prior-tick state, default to safe-skip rather than safe-execute. PM2 restart at t=0 + halt at t=-5s + first post-restart tick at t=+10s = gap-down resume price executable through shouldClosePosition without the fail-safe. Cost: one tick of stop-check delay per symbol per cold-start episode. Trivial operationally; load-bearing for correctness.

5. **Pre-existing CI rot must be diagnosed before being attributed.** Initial scope of "CI cleanup batch" was premised on the assumption that today's B-NEW-40 morning work introduced ~20 TS errors + 13 test failures. Investigation showed: TypeScript Check has been `continue-on-error: true` since 2026-03-30 (non-blocking by design); Test Suite has been red for at least 10 days; pre-existing Directive 11.3/11.7F era code refactored without updating its tests. Kyle accepted as documented technical debt. Lesson: when surfacing CI status as an issue, ALWAYS verify the attribution (check `gh run list` history + CI workflow `continue-on-error` flags) before declaring scope. The corrected attribution went into RUNNING_ISSUES #113 the same session it was discovered.

All 8 Langston Step 8 second-pass checks PASS. Three mandatory disclaimers in completion report (pre-current-bundle / root-cause-unverified / op-hygiene flag) per Langston Step 2 ACK iteration.

**Mid-batch architectural finding 2026-05-21 PM (Kyle directive):** the hardcoded-registry pattern itself is a recurring structural cost — crypto auto-discovers from Kraken's REST `AssetPairs` endpoint (live ~1,544 pairs every cycle), but Kraken's public REST API does not index xStock instruments at all, forcing manual registry maintenance that scales poorly as Kraken adds more tokenized stocks. **Locked decision:** insert NEW sub-batch `B79.0n.UNIVERSE-DISCOVERY` as #2 (between HYGIENE and STORAGE). Umbrella v2 → v3 with sub-batch count 17 → 18; STORAGE shifts 2→3, all subsequent +1.

**Sub-batch 2 — B79.0n.UNIVERSE-DISCOVERY NEXT (scope drafting 2026-05-21 PM).** Replaces hardcoded registry with dynamically-populated universe sourced from a three-service chain: (1) CoinGecko tokenized-stocks category for "what's Backed Finance issuing today" (CoinGecko already wired in `external-macro-feed.ts` for `/global` endpoint), (2) Kraken WebSocket subscription probe to confirm which of those Kraken's WS-equities feed actually accepts, (3) external equity metadata source (Finnhub already wired in `stocks.ts` — key may need re-provisioning) for sector / cryptoAdjacent / ADR flag lookup per symbol. DB-cached snapshot with daily refresh + ad-hoc trigger. Fallback chain on service unavailability: last-known-good snapshot → small hard-coded bootstrap set → fail-fast at boot. Test infrastructure converts exact-equality size asserts to range asserts so universe growth doesn't break tests. Pre-WIRE-IN dependency: STORAGE (foundational asset-class-awareness batch) executes AFTER UNIVERSE-DISCOVERY so it inherits the dynamic registry.

**Open universe-discovery learnings captured for Phase 24 end-of-arc consolidation** (per CLAUDE.md §3.3, in HYGIENE completion report §5):
- Generic pattern: every asset class needs an explicit "live universe discovery source chain" documented up-front. For classes where the exchange exposes a list-all REST endpoint (crypto, perpetual futures via `https://futures.kraken.com/derivatives/api/v3/instruments`), the registry is a thin live-cache. For classes where it doesn't (xStock), the discovery chain must be built BEFORE the registry is treated as load-bearing.
- Test-pattern recommendation: prefer `size >= MIN && size <= MAX` range assertions over `size === N` exact-equality. Same drift-guard effect; doesn't break when the universe legitimately grows/shrinks.
- Boot-time round-trip smoke test pattern (set → get → assert literal → reset, with `process.exit(1)` on failure) is reusable for ANY shared-utility helper that's heavily call-site-imported. Catches bundling drift early and loudly.

**Sub-batches 3-18 sequence:** STORAGE → MCE → STRATEGY → PATTERN-DETECT → CONFIDENCE-CHAIN → SCORING → TEC → TELEMETRY → RTB → RTB-REFRESH → POOL → ORCHESTRATOR → EXECUTION → WIRE-IN; Tier 2: ML-CALIBRATION + OBSERVABILITY. Same per-batch standing rules from umbrella §2 apply throughout.

Poll path skips prewarm (catch-up semantics) and writes a system-alert (severity=warning, category=breakage) so cron regressions surface to §10.5 per-turn checks rather than silently relying on poll forever. `[B-NEW-36][POLL_RECONCILE_CHECK]` 10-min heartbeat provides positive proof-of-life. 9 new unit tests cover all states + race + mutex-unlock-on-throw + skip-prewarm + alert shape + state-already-matches no-op. Langston Design ACK-W-REVISIONS (3 structural folded) + Step-4 ACK clean (heartbeat minor folded). **Honest scope:** root cause of cron silent failure remains UNKNOWN. Fix is robust regardless because the poll-reconcile rides on the central-clock path that has positive evidence of firing reliably. **B-NEW-50 CLOSED 2026-06-01** — node-cron next-fire readout fix ([completion report](../Claude%20Comms%20and%20Packages/Batch%20Completion/B_NEW_50_COMPLETION_REPORT.md)). Resolves RUNNING_ISSUES #165 (surfaced by B-NEW-49's smoke test). Root cause proven: node-cron 4.2.1 `MatcherWalker.matchNext()` advances the weekday-reconcile loop by a whole YEAR per iteration → ANY day-of-week schedule ≥~2 days out returns a future Jan-1st (Fri→2027, Tue→2030…), both NY+UTC — **broader than the original Friday-NY framing**. PROVEN **introspection-only**: firing uses a separate `TimeMatcher.match(now)` predicate + 24h heartbeat-delay cap, self-correcting as the fire-time nears (live dow-fire test + the Sun 2026-06-01 00:00 UTC `weekend_restart` firing via cron, src=cron, both confirm). The May 29 non-fire was the 30h outage, not this bug. Fix = SHIM: new `cron-next-fire.ts` `computeNextFire()` via `cron-parser` (direct dep @4.9.0); arm-logger + smoke-test classify on it; node-cron raw value kept as `[UNTRUSTED ncv=4.2.1]` diagnostic only; scheduling/firing untouched. Staging-verified: smoke `aggregate=OK 7/7`, weekend timers next_fire 2026-06-06/2026-06-08; 4 stale alerts acked. **BUG-2026-06-01-A:** first deploy (`6372a2d`) crash-looped — named import of CJS `cron-parser` fails the prod esbuild ESM bundle; hotfix `63bc69d` (default-import), ~3-min outage, no trades affected. CI green both runs. Langston Step-1/4/8 CONFIRMED. Spawned RUNNING_ISSUES #168 (CI lacks a production-bundle boot smoke). Active trading OFF throughout.

**B-NEW-51 CLOSED 2026-06-02** — cron-fire-evidence verifier: cadence-aware staleness + root-level alert dedup ([completion report](../Claude%20Comms%20and%20Packages/Batch%20Completion/B_NEW_51_COMPLETION_REPORT.md)). Deploy `c7529f146`; CI run `26830180190` all-4-green; HTTP 200; PM2 #344. **Kills the every-15-min `weekend_shutdown` stale-alert spam Kyle flagged (16+ alerts in one afternoon, each auto-routed to Langston → alert desensitization).** Two structural root causes, both fixed (NO-PATCHES): (1) the B-NEW-49 verifier judged staleness as `lastFire + intervalSeconds×1.5` — calendar-blind, so for the weekly `0 20 * * 5` it set `expected_by = 2026-05-23 + 10.5d = 2026-06-02T12:00Z`, i.e. it expected the WEEKEND timer to have fired by TUESDAY → fix = NEW `computePrevFire` (cron-parser `.prev()`, same trusted path as B-NEW-50's computeNextFire) so the verifier compares against the schedule's ACTUAL most-recent calendar occurrence (stale iff `lastFire < prevOccurrence − 10-min latency grace`; 5-min process boot-grace; interval×1.5 fallback only for unparseable exprs). (2) `addAlert` had NO dedup → the 15-min re-check re-created the same alert + re-invoked Langston each cycle → fix = root-level optional `dedupe_key` (suppress a new alert when a NON-terminal — scheduled/active/acknowledged — same-key alert exists; `resolved` does NOT block; backward-compatible, benefits ALL alert sources). Verifier passes `cron_stale:<job>:<prevOccurrence ISO>` → exactly ONE alert per genuinely-missed occurrence, auto-clears on next successful fire. **Important reconciliation (Langston live-diagnosis):** the symptom Langston first attributed to "the Jan-2027 startup miscalculation" was already fixed in B-NEW-50 — the live noise was the fire-evidence verifier's own cadence-blindness + repeat-spam, not a firing failure; the 05-29/30 miss is genuine but pre-dates the fixes with no weekend since, so it can only re-confirm on the June 5-6 weekend (poll-reconcile B-NEW-36 protects the outcome regardless; active trading OFF). 13 legacy `weekend_shutdown` alerts resolved post-deploy; live one-then-zero confirmation on the next ~15-min verifier cycle. tsc 493 baseline (0 net new); 28/28 targeted tests (cron-next-fire prev cases + cadence-aware verifier + NEW system-alerts-dedup suite). Langston Step-1 + Step-4 approved (both grace constants + dedup contract confirmed; documented contracts: resolve-while-broken re-surfaces [intentional], newly-registered-cron `no_fires_ever` edge → RUNNING_ISSUES). Bundled: B.2.UI cosmetic (whole-number crypto quantities, commit `39c5e578c`). Active trading OFF throughout.

Kyle's ruling, now a standing lesson: findings that corrupt a new system's learning data get FIXED before moving on, never parked as side notes. All three resolved + a 4th gap the pre-audit surfaced: (#222) the crypto DBS store's equity contamination traced to a single un-class-gated write site from the B63 era — now crypto-allowlisted, with a permanent registry-based purity probe in the audit script (post-restart refill verified pure); (#223) crossed/stale ticker quotes can no longer write negative spreads into the cost data — field-level guard at the single write chokepoint, fabricating nothing on a first bad write; it caught 18 real bad quotes in its first ten minutes live; (#224) the AMR weather now honestly reads IDLE during friction warm-up instead of a thin-input CALM, and (the 4th gap) under an ACTIVE flag a restart no longer has an ungated window — no weather read means entries are blocked (`no_posture`), exits never gated. Deployed `5737b1ddb` at **01:01:56Z** — a recorded boundary timestamp: DBS-stamped learning rows before/after it carry the contaminated/clean crypto aggregate, and the AMR shadow-week review must attribute any step-change at that instant to the fix. 13/13 audit legs PASS post-deploy; Langston full-cycle (scope ACK → pre-audit pins → Step-4 APPROVE → reader-walk addendum OK-DEPLOY → Step-8 CONFIRMED, catching one evidence miss: the guard's log lives in error.log, not out.log). Process lesson recorded in CHANGES_AND_FIXES: enumerate, don't assert — any component outside the batch's fresh docs gets the explicit SIM walk regardless of diff size. Next: B-4.6-B chunk-B scan-stall fix, then Phase 19.

**B-4.6-B chunk B shipped 2026-06-12 — the scan stall is ELIMINATED (readiness item 4.6 closed) ✅** (scope `B_4_6B_SCAN_STALL_SCOPE.md` · catalog row for the full iteration inventory). The system used to freeze for a fifth to half a second on every 30-second sweep — the source of the missed scheduled jobs. The fix landed in two parts, each Langston-reviewed before push: first, the scan loops now take sub-millisecond cooperative breathers between pairs whenever 20 milliseconds of uninterrupted work accumulates (never mid-pair — the locked granularity rule lives in the SIM), which collapsed every measured compute segment to tiny slices while leaving scan outputs byte-identical; second — and decisively — the measurement escalation (GC observer, two extra loop wraps, then a stall watchdog that timestamps any blockage) proved the DOMINANT freeze was never compute at all: a Batch-44 housekeeping routine was synchronously rewriting a 20-30MB diagnostics file to disk every cycle, with 1.6GB of unread daily files accumulated. **Kyle's ruling: legacy — delete, don't optimize.** The whole disk layer was removed (no consumer existed anywhere, proven independently by CC and Langston); scan diagnostics are now in-memory only. Evidence (corrected at Langston's second pass, which caught CC's first figures as windowed extrapolations): before, every measured minute had a 229-574ms freeze (~120 per hour); after, brief one-off hiccups roughly eight times an hour (median ~0.2s, worst ~0.55s) with steady-state latency excellent (worst p99 26.61ms). TWO honest corrections on record: the original soak attributed the spike to the price-download loop (partial — corrected in FIX-2026-06-12-C), and CC's first close-out counts were wrong until Langston's full-window read fixed them. The batch closes with its formal gate PARTIAL — the smoothness target (p99) passes with huge margin; the no-spike-over-250ms clause fails on the residual hiccup family, now #225 with a quantified baseline and the permanent stall watchdog as tripwire. **Kyle decision RESOLVED same day: gate amendment ACCEPTED (2026-06-12)** — the speed clause stands as passed; the spike clause is replaced by a pinned ceiling at the day's measured residual baseline, with the watchdog tripping on any growth (justification recorded: the original clause's one-cause assumption was factually wrong; not a near-miss waiver). Formal 24-hour read fires on a scheduled alert Jun 13 and rules on the amended gate. The interphase plan is COMPLETE — next: Phase 19 (turn Paper Mode Active Trading back on).

**P19-B1 shipped 2026-06-13 — PHASE 19 IS UNDERWAY: test-suite cleanup, the first Phase-19 batch, CLOSED ✅** (scope `P19_B1_SCOPE.md` · catalog row for the full bucket inventory · plan home `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §1). The suite went from 12 failures + 141 skips (bench) to **0 failed / 0 skipped on BOTH the bench and CI (1880/1880 tests, 161/161 files)** — every future red is now a real signal, which was the batch's whole purpose as the Phase-19 debugging enabler. Headline correction on the books (§9.2): the long-carried "59/12 pre-existing failures" figure was FALSE — CI has been zero-tolerance green since B-NEW-43; all bench red was environment (no local DB; fixed via the Kyle-approved Docker Desktop install + a CI-mirror database blueprint in the repo) plus latent bugs, two of them genuine production-tooling finds (the migration runner's Windows drive-doubling; a stateful-regex guard weakener in the regime-string scan that could silently skip violations on ANY platform — guard-the-guard verified with a planted violation). The June-9-parked TEC.b strictness restore SHIPPED (#141 closed): all 11 exit-system config keys now hard-fail loudly if missing instead of silently running on built-in defaults, with the blast radius MEASURED on the parity bench before repair (exactly the predicted +50), 8 stale test fixtures repaired, a 5-test strict regression lock (incl. a 12th-key tripwire), and the obsolete soft-contract test REWRITTEN to lock the new contract. Deploy-verified live: all 4 asset classes' exit config warmed in 29ms, zero strict-mode throws; the weekend state survived the restart by design (30s poll-reconcile — Sunday restart structurally safe). 7 parked-stale skips DELETED with replacement coverage verified first; NEW #226 opened (unit/integration tier separation — the structural fix for CI's DB container masking unit-test hermeticity violations). Langston gates: Step-1 ACK → Step-2 PROCEED → Step-4 APPROVE → Step-8 CONFIRMED. Kyle's Phase-19 working-style directive (code-level audits, certainty-before-deletion, CC+Langston autonomous to verified-correct) applied end-to-end. Next: P19-B2 (live-mode build-approach decision) per the locked plan.

**P19-B2 shipped 2026-06-13 — the live-mode build direction is DECIDED, and a piece of dead leftover live machinery is gone ✅** (scope `P19_B2_SCOPE.md` · catalog row for the full inventory · plan home `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §1/§5). This was a short design-decision batch — no live trading was built or turned on; real-money trading stays locked off until Phase 21. **The decision:** when real-money trading is eventually built, it will be built ON TOP of the existing paper-trading engine (which was already written to run in either mode), not as a separate engine — the only genuine difference is the single moment an order is placed and closed. Building a separate engine would mean re-testing thousands of lines we'll have just hardened, which our no-shortcuts rule forbids. Kyle approved this with conditions (paper and live will run side-by-side once live works, each with its own clearly-labeled page and trade tables), and Langston added two real-money safety items to keep clean while we fix paper: real orders don't fill the way pretend ones do (they can fill partly or get rejected), and real trading sizes bets against the actual account balance — both now scheduled into the upcoming wire-in batch, along with a check that paper and live can never interfere with each other's risk budgets. **A significant correction landed:** Kyle wanted paper trading to route through Kraken's own paper-trading system rather than our own simulation, so we investigated exhaustively (Kraken's own help pages, blog, Reddit, GitHub) — and Kraken simply does NOT offer a paper-trading system for spot crypto/xStock (their hosted practice account is futures-only; even their own new command-line tool simulates spot paper locally). Kyle's memory of an advertised spot paper system traces to that futures demo amid 2025's heavy derivatives marketing. So paper stays our own simulation, but upgraded to be genuinely Kraken-vetted (every order checked by the real exchange first, priced off the real feed, with honest fees and slippage) — a real step up from the passive learning system. The governance wording that wrongly said "paper routes through Kraken" was corrected, and the dead end was logged so nobody re-investigates it. **Finally**, on Kyle's directive to stop leaving legacy code lingering, the old half-built "live trading" file — a do-nothing placeholder that, worse, broadcast a false "live is ON" signal — was deleted entirely (along with its four unused endpoints, a dead approval branch, and a stale reference), verified clean (typecheck + all 1,854 tests green, the real go-live button and the page untouched), reviewed by Langston, and recorded in a new permanent deleted-components log with an archived copy. That instituted a new standing rule: legacy code gets deleted on the spot or scheduled for a dated deletion — never left lingering. CI run `27469343597` all-4-green; deployed `59d501fc4`. Next: P19-B3 (repair the known-broken parts of the active trading path), which opens by building the clean order-placement boundary this batch identified.

**P19-B3 shipped 2026-06-14 — the active trading path's known-broken parts are repaired, in two halves ✅** (scope `P19_B3_SCOPE.md` · `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §1/§5). B3a built the clean order-placement boundary (the `OrderPlacer` port), widened the crypto symbol-length ceiling, and centralized the asset-class-classify alarm. B3b triaged the active-path TypeScript errors by blast-radius — FIXED the 13 active-path files (474→404 typecheck, CI baseline gate green) and HOMED the ~390 off-path errors (#234, baseline-suppressed). The critical find was a silent signal-killer: the signal orchestrator was building incomplete signal objects that threw inside a fire-and-forget catch, which would have silently dropped EVERY signal the moment active-paper turned on — fixed with required risk fields + an observable failure counter, and a dead database-column write removed (Langston-confirmed nothing reads it). New homes #231/#232/#233 (pre-go-live verifies) + #234. Langston split-gate + Step-4 both APPROVE. CI `27481700174` all-4-green; deployed `d9b312780`, clean boot, suite 1899/1899. Next: P19-B4 (xStock active wire-in + paper fill fidelity), split into B4a + B4b.

**P19-B4a shipped 2026-06-14 — the xStock side is wired into the active-paper machinery and made feed-safe, all DORMANT until activation ✅** (scope `P19_B4a_SCOPE.md` · `P19_B4a_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` · `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §1/§5/§6). Eight chunks: stamp-at-source asset-class labeling (single source of truth, collision-correct for the 17 tickers that exist as BOTH xStock and crypto); the xStock active-dispatch wire-in (gated OFF until B7b — §9.1 scaffolding); a three-part fill-safety gate (price-freshness + a liquid-fill-window + a silent-stall watchdog, all DB-resolved fail-closed); classify hardening with an escalation hook; a DB-resolved per-strategy active gate (default-open) that disposed a hardcoded strategy list; a calibration-state tag on the paper-sim tables; an interim pattern-cap correction (0.50→0.15). Deferrals homed: #237 (RTB SET-NOT-NULL → B7b real soak), #238 (strategy-gate seed → B7b pre-flight), #236 (holiday-liveness gate → new B6.6). Langston Step-4 APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS (all ratified) + Step-8 CONFIRMED-CLOSED (independently re-verified on staging). Deployed code `1523ff7ac` (CI `27517345398` all-4-green, 3 migrations applied); governance head `659b8c60e`. **Design note (Kyle 2026-06-15):** the liquid-fill-window was an RTH time-of-day PROXY for book liquidity, used only because no live order-book-depth feed existed yet; it is being REPLACED by a direct book-depth-sufficiency gate that works 24/5 once the depth feed lands in B4b.1 (#295). Next: P19-B4b (paper fill fidelity + paper/live split-brain isolation).

**P19-B4b D5 shipped 2026-06-15 — paper and live trading can no longer step on each other's shared memory ✅** (run autonomously with Langston per Kyle's directive · `P19_B4b_D5_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` · `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §5). This is the safety groundwork for the future where paper and live trading run at the same time (Phase 21): today they share a handful of in-memory objects, and if both ran at once one mode would overwrite the other's open-position list, risk ceilings, and "is trading on?" flag. Fixed by giving each mode its own copy of the four that mattered — the portfolio manager that holds the position/exposure/drawdown ceilings (the worst one), the risk-concentration weights, the order-refresh latches, and a single source of truth for the engine-on flag (which previously updated a tick late and let five different readers disagree). The engine-on flag now writes to the database FIRST and only then announces the change, and a background self-check raises an observable alarm if those readers ever disagree — that alarm reading clean during a paper+live trial is the hard gate before the two are ever run together. A dead start/stop locking mechanism left over from an old design was deleted outright. Nothing changes for today's behavior (paper still runs identically; live stays off). Two design calls were sharpened with Langston mid-batch: the shared trading-venue rate limiter was deferred to its own batch because its file is production-locked AND, on closer look, it should be keyed by the account credential rather than the mode (paper and live share one Kraken key, so a real venue lockout SHOULD freeze both). Bench fully green (typecheck clean + 1,952 tests). Langston Step-4 APPROVE after a changes-needed round that caught a missing test for the new self-check alarm (added) and a file-transfer that silently failed (re-sent). Next: P19-B4b.1 (the higher-fidelity fill model, which needs a live order-book-depth feed) + the deferred rate-limiter batch; B6.5 (crypto active-path proof) and B6.6 (holiday-liveness gate) still gate turn-on.

**P19-B4b.1 shipped 2026-06-16 — paper trades now fill against the real order book instead of a flat made-up slippage ✅** (run autonomously with Langston per Kyle's directive · `P19_B4b_1_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` · `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §1/§5/§6). The audit found the depth data was already there but unused — crypto has a live ten-level order book streaming in, and xStock already measures how much is available at the best price — so this batch connected that to the fill logic. A paper buy now "walks the book" (takes the best prices first, gets a worse average as it eats into the book); if the order is bigger than what's available it fills partially and the position is sized down to exactly what filled, so the system can never overstate how much it holds. A paper sell always completes (you can always get out of a market, just at a worse price in a thin book), avoiding a stuck-position edge case. It also delivered Kyle's call from the prior week: the temporary daytime-hours fill gate is retired and replaced by a real, around-the-clock "is there enough depth to fill this cleanly" check, with the thresholds set from live measurements (the big coins refresh many times a second with deep books; thin names briefly go shallow — which is exactly where the new check should bite). Two honest course-corrections during the work: the audit caught that xStock isn't actually depth-blind (so it got a real check, not a weaker fallback), and a cleanup nearly deleted some old daytime-window code until the verification sweep caught that a separate price-feed-silence monitor still legitimately uses it — so it was kept and only its use as a fill gate removed. One piece is deliberately deferred to its own batch: actually sending each paper order to the exchange to be vetted, because that's tangled up with the production-locked rate-limit code. Nothing changes for today's behavior — it all stays switched off until the later turn-on. Bench green (typecheck clean + 1,979 tests), CI all-4-green, deployed and verified on staging (the new settings seeded correctly). Langston reviewed and approved every gate. Next: the dead-code sweep (P19-B4b.2) before turn-on, plus B6.5 (crypto active-path proof) and B6.6 (holiday-liveness gate) which still gate it.

**P19-B4b.2 shipped 2026-06-16 — the old, dead leftover piece of paper-trading fill machinery is gone, so it can never accidentally wake up once paper trading is turned back on ✅** (run autonomously with Langston per Kyle's directive · `P19_B4b_2_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` · `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §1/§5). Before cutting anything I traced exactly what still touched the dead piece, and it turned out a little more tangled than the earlier note suggested — so the cut was done carefully. The main deletion was a half-built, never-finished paper-trade executor left over from a 2025-era design (its actual trade function had no callers and its record-keeping step was never wired up); it was removed entirely and archived. Two small internal status readouts that still lightly referenced it were quietly re-pointed at the live sources they should have read all along — no behavior change, just the dead middleman removed; Langston checked line-by-line that the numbers come out identical. A dead diagnostic web address that nothing in the app used was also removed (the working one next to it was left alone), along with a duplicate, unused order-book storage path. Two honest course-corrections came out of the verification sweep: the earlier note called these "three clean cuts," but the trace showed the second piece (a redundant market-data connection) is actually shared live infrastructure, so only the genuinely-dead part of it was removed and the live part kept; and that same redundant connection turns out to have stopped being used long ago and is just sitting idle — too big to remove safely in this pass, so it was written up as its own scheduled cleanup (#301) rather than swept in here. Nothing turns on and no fill behavior changes — it is purely removing code that no longer does anything. Bench green (typecheck no-regression + 1,979 tests), CI all-4-green, deployed and verified on staging (the removed address now correctly returns "not found"). Next: the deferred order-vetting / rate-limiter batch (#296), plus B6.5 (crypto active-path proof) and B6.6 (holiday-liveness gate) which still gate turn-on.

**P19-B5a shipped 2026-06-16 — the system now keeps a record of *why* each coin gets rejected at every stage of the live trading pipeline, ready for when active trading turns back on ✅** (run autonomously with Langston per Kyle's directive · `P19_B5a_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` · `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §1/§5). Until now the system only recorded the coins it *accepted* on the active path; the reasons coins got screened out were thrown away. This batch adds small record-keeping notes at every rejection point — the screening filters (too thin, too cheap, spread too wide, and the deeper liquidity/trend-quality checks), the quality-score reject, the ready-to-buy re-check, and the duplicate-position guard — plus a final "position actually opened" record. Each note captures the real number that caused the decision (the failing quality score, the confidence that fell short) rather than a blank, so the data is usable for tuning later. It is pure record-keeping: it changes nothing about which coins get traded, and every note is written in a way that can never interfere with or slow down a trading decision. Critically, all of it stays completely silent until active paper trading is switched on (the later turn-on batch) — verified live on staging, where zero records appeared because the system is still in its current learning-only mode. Two judgment calls were made and confirmed with Langston: one "rejection" that's really just a *deferral* when the system is at its trade limit was deliberately not recorded (recording it would be misleading — those coins aren't rejected, just waiting); and one quality number that simply isn't available that far down the pipeline was left blank with a documented way to recover it by cross-referencing the earlier record. A careful pre-check before writing any code caught that three of the originally-planned recording spots pointed at the wrong lines (one was leftover code that no longer rejects anything, one merely re-counts already-rejected coins, and one real rejection had been missed) — all corrected and re-approved before a single line was written. Bench green (typecheck no-regression + 1,987 tests including 8 new), CI all-4-green, deployed and verified. Langston reviewed the full change and independently confirmed the records stay silent until turn-on. Next in this group: B5b (attaching a market-wide backdrop snapshot to each xStock decision) and B5c (a continuous quality probe).

**P19-B5b shipped 2026-06-16 — every tokenized-stock decision is now stamped with the broader market backdrop (the fear gauge and the dollar index), so the system has that context recorded for tuning later ✅** (run autonomously with Langston per Kyle's directive · `P19_B5b_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` · `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §1/§5). This is the data-gathering groundwork for a planned future feature (in Phase 25) that will let the xStock side grade its confidence by the overall market mood — calmer or more fearful markets, a stronger or weaker dollar. That feature isn't built here; this batch just makes sure the raw ingredients are being recorded at the moment of each decision, because you can't go back and reconstruct what the market looked like at a past instant. Each xStock decision record now carries a small snapshot: the fear-gauge and dollar-index readings (both the headline "how unusual is this vs normal" number AND the raw values, since the raw ones are the only ground truth that survives if the "normal" baseline is later recalculated), plus how fresh that reading was and whether one of the two feeds was lagging. Two careful touches confirmed with Langston: a market-closed blank is recorded as an explicit blank that stays distinct from a genuine zero (they mean different things), and the staleness of the reading is captured so a six-hour-old number during a market-closed window can never be mistaken for a live one. Unlike the previous piece, this one is NOT silent — it starts recording the instant it ships, because it just adds one field to a record the system already writes every cycle; that was flagged clearly throughout and it changes nothing about which trades happen. The recording logic was pulled into its own small file purely so it could be tested in isolation. Verified live on the server: brand-new xStock records came through with the backdrop snapshot populated and sensible. Bench green (typecheck no-regression + 1,992 tests including 5 new), CI all-4-green, deployed and confirmed writing. Langston reviewed and approved the full change. Next: B5c (the continuous quality probe).

**P19-B5c shipped 2026-06-16 — the system now continuously logs how expensive each tokenized stock is to trade, building the long-term cost record needed to model trading friction properly later ✅** (run autonomously with Langston per Kyle's directive · `P19_B5c_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` · `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §1/§5). A new always-on watcher wakes every five minutes and records, for each of the ~490 tokenized stocks the system tracks, how wide the gap is between the going buy and sell price, how much size is available at the best price on each side, and how fresh that reading is — saving it into its own dedicated history table kept for ninety days. The point: trading-cost modeling needs the distribution of costs over days and weeks, and the raw price feed the system already keeps gets thinned out and shipped to cold storage too quickly to serve that. This batch only COLLECTS that evidence; actually using it to set per-stock cost assumptions is deliberately left for later (Phase 25). A few careful design choices, all confirmed with Langston: each five-minute slot is labelled by the clock time the watcher ran (not the timestamp on the price reading) so a feed gap shows up as an honest run of stale readings rather than collapsing into one; quotes that are missing a side or inverted are stored with the raw numbers but flagged distinctly (including the "price but no size available" case); a weekend — when the feed pauses — is made distinguishable from an actual breakage in the watcher's own run-log; and the ninety-day cleanup of old rows was folded into the existing nightly cleanup job rather than inventing a new one. The separate "tokenized vs. real underlying stock price" comparison was deliberately kept out and parked for the later cost-modeling work, since the real stock only trades during US market hours while the tokenized version trades around the clock. Verification earned its keep here: the very first real run hit a thirty-second database timeout because the original query scanned the entire (very large) raw price archive instead of using the index — exactly the risk Langston had flagged in code review — so it was rewritten to look up each stock individually via the index, after which a run completed in about three seconds and wrote all 486 readable stocks with sensible spreads (around 0.15% typical). Bench green (typecheck no-regression + 2,006 tests including 14 new), CI all-4-green, deployed and live-verified. Langston independently reproduced the results on the server and confirmed. This completes the B5 group (B5a record-keeping, B5b market-backdrop, B5c cost probe).

**P19-B6 shipped 2026-06-17 — the automatic "stop trading if the day's losses get too deep" safety brake is back, this time with early-warning alerts and a clean restart ✅** (run autonomously with Langston per Kyle's directive · `P19_B6_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` · `PHASE_19_PLAN.md` §1/§5/§6). Kyle remembered this protection had worked back in Phase 8, so the first job was to check rather than assume — and he was right: the system once had an automatic daily-loss cutoff that quietly got deleted at the start of the year as collateral of an unrelated cleanup. So this batch RESTORED it rather than building something new. The brake watches the running loss over the trailing 24 hours as a percentage of the account, and if it crosses the user's set limit it automatically halts trading and closes every open position — using the system's existing stop-and-flatten path, so there's no risk of double-closing. On top of the hard cutoff there are now two early-warning alerts at the halfway and three-quarter marks of that limit, so the user gets a heads-up before the brake actually trips. Both the warnings and the cutoff show up in two places: the operational alert feed (and phone) AND a new dismissible banner right on the website that the user can click away. The limit and the two warning levels are user settings, and — per Kyle's call — there's now a separate set for paper mode and live mode, with strict sanity-checking so the warning levels can never be entered out of order. The window resets on a restart (a deliberate "circuit-breaker" so a fresh start gets a clean slate, not a lock-out until tomorrow). Langston's code review caught two real bugs before they shipped: the warning-order sanity check was comparing the numbers as text (so "9" looked bigger than "80"), and a failure mid-shutdown could have permanently jammed the brake in the off position — both fixed, the second one now self-recovers and raises a loud alert if a shutdown ever fails. The trip-and-recovery was proven with a forced-failure test; its real exercise on live closing trades comes when paper trading is switched on (the later turn-on batch), so today it's built and verified but dormant. One honest limit: like the Phase-8 original, it only counts realized (booked) losses, not paper losses on still-open positions — that gap is written up and scheduled for Phase 25. A leftover unused copy of the old loss-calculation helper was deleted. Bench green (typecheck no-regression + 23 tests), CI all-4-green, deployed and verified on staging (warning levels seeded on both modes, clean boot). Langston reviewed, re-approved after the two fixes, and independently confirmed on the server. A follow-on batch was scheduled (P19-B6.8) to give paper and live their own full separate set of all the other user guardrails too. Next: per the Phase-19 sequence.
